Expanded study · 15 November 2026 · No Node required

Low false alarms.
Useful error detection.

607 labeled JavaScript samples, seven Python-deployable configurations, independent challenges, and an explicit recommendation that weighs maintenance as well as accuracy.

ConclusionResults MethodPerformance & limitsMaintenance Python snippetEvidence
01 / The final choice

Best overall balance: Acorn, hosted by Python.

For this application, choose pinned Acorn 8.19.0 inside mini-racer==0.14.1. The container needs Python and its native wheel, not Node.js. Execute the trusted parser and pass generated JavaScript as data; never evaluate the submitted program.

The priority is to avoid flagging valid code as broken, while tolerating occasional missed edge cases. On the expanded corpus Acorn-V8 wrongly rejected 0/302 valid samples and missed 1/305 invalid samples. Its parser has substantial adoption, and the V8 route handled our depth-300 valid probe. This is a practical tradeoff, not a mathematical proof or an unconditional winner for every workload.

This default assumes a persistent worker and an acceptable native-engine footprint: the V8 worker peaked at approximately 103 MiB RSS in this run. If that memory budget is too high, the QuickJS host's approximately 33 MiB observed peak makes it the better deployment tradeoff, with its tested default-stack limitation handled as inconclusive.

Why not simply choose Tree-sitter? Low false alarms alone do not favor it: Tree-sitter rejected 3/302 valid samples, but missed 259/305 invalid samples. It remains an excellent fast structural/advisory screen. Once useful error detection is included, Acorn catches substantially more without a measured false-alarm penalty in this corpus.
Why not the pure-Python fork? esprima-fork==5.0.0 recorded 0 false rejects and 0 missed invalid cases. Credit that result. However, its snapshot shows 0 repository stars, 129 monthly distribution downloads and a roughly four-year gap preceding the latest release. It also reaches resource limits on deeply nested valid inputs. It is the strongest tested pure-Python alternative, but its independent scrutiny and maintenance continuity are less established. The recommendation deliberately trades a disclosed Acorn edge-case miss for stronger ecosystem evidence.

Choose the host deliberately

Acorn in QuickJS-NG has the same parser logic, a smaller deployment footprint and a more recently released wrapper. It is a credible alternative when size matters. The V8 route is the default here for broader listed platform support, greater wrapper adoption and better depth tolerance in our tests—not because two hosts are two independent validators. mini-racer's latest release was February 2026; its age is a real maintenance consideration, not hidden by V8's reputation.

Acceptance policy: a parser-reported syntax error is a candidate for repair only under the correct Script/Module and language-version contract. Timeouts, unsupported configurations, resource exhaustion and crashes are inconclusive, not broken JavaScript. If avoiding false rejection dominates everything, make uncertain outcomes advisory. Syntax acceptance still does not guarantee correct rendering.

02 / Measured classification

“False positive” in the user's terminology means valid code wrongly flagged as broken; here it is called a false rejection. A false acceptance means invalid code slipped through.

One important Acorn miss

class C { static { const f = () => arguments; } }

This is invalid: class static-block ContainsArguments checks descend through arrows. The corresponding ordinary function may use its own arguments. Both Acorn hosts accept this pinned-version counterexample; the fork rejects it. An independent reviewer confirmed the expected label against ES2025 ContainsArguments. No untested patch is quietly applied to inflate Acorn's score.

test262-language--arguments-object--async-gen-meth-args-trailing-comma-spread-operator

Expected: valid; goal: script

// This file was procedurally generated from the following sources:
// - src/arguments/args-trailing-comma-spread-operator.case
// - src/arguments/default/async-gen-meth.template
/*---
description: A trailing comma should not increase the arguments.length, using spread args (async generator method)
esid: sec-asyncgenerator-definitions-propertydefinitionevaluation
features: [async-iteration]
flags: [generated, async]
info: |
    AsyncGeneratorMethod :
        async [no LineTerminator here] * PropertyName ( UniqueFormalParameters )
            { AsyncGeneratorBody }

    1. Let propKey be the result of evaluating PropertyName.
    2. ReturnIfAbrupt(propKey).
    3. If the function code for this AsyncGeneratorMethod is strict mode code, let strict be true.
       Otherwise let strict be false.
    4. Let scope be the running execution context's LexicalEnvironment.
    5. Let closure be ! AsyncGeneratorFunctionCreate(Method, UniqueFormalParameters,
       AsyncGeneratorBody, scope, strict).
    [...]


    Trailing comma in the arguments list

    Left-Hand-Side Expressions

    Arguments :
        ( )
        ( ArgumentList )
        ( ArgumentList , )

    ArgumentList :
        AssignmentExpression
        ... AssignmentExpression
        ArgumentList , AssignmentExpression
        ArgumentList , ... AssignmentExpression
---*/
var arr = [2, 3];


var callCount = 0;
var obj = {
  async *method() {
    assert.sameValue(arguments.length, 4);
    assert.sameValue(arguments[0], 42);
    assert.sameValue(arguments[1], 1);
    assert.sameValue(arguments[2], 2);
    assert.sameValue(arguments[3], 3);
    callCount = callCount + 1;
  }
};

// Stores a reference `ref` for case evaluation
var ref = obj.method;

ref(42, ...[1], ...arr,).next().then(() => {
    assert.sameValue(callCount, 1, 'generator method invoked exactly once');
}).then($DONE, $DONE);

test262-language--expressions--class--elements--new-no-sc-line-method-rs-static-privatename-identifier

Expected: valid; goal: script

// This file was procedurally generated from the following sources:
// - src/class-elements/rs-static-privatename-identifier.case
// - src/class-elements/productions/cls-expr-new-no-sc-line-method.template
/*---
description: Valid Static PrivateName (field definitions followed by a method in a new line without a semicolon)
esid: prod-FieldDefinition
features: [class-static-fields-private, class, class-fields-public]
flags: [generated]
includes: [propertyHelper.js]
info: |
    ClassElement :
      MethodDefinition
      static MethodDefinition
      FieldDefinition ;
      static FieldDefinition ;
      ;

    FieldDefinition :
      ClassElementName Initializer _opt

    ClassElementName :
      PropertyName
      PrivateName

    PrivateName ::
      # IdentifierName

    IdentifierName ::
      IdentifierStart
      IdentifierName IdentifierPart

    IdentifierStart ::
      UnicodeIDStart
      $
      _
      \ UnicodeEscapeSequence

    IdentifierPart::
      UnicodeIDContinue
      $
      \ UnicodeEscapeSequence
      <ZWNJ> <ZWJ>

    UnicodeIDStart::
      any Unicode code point with the Unicode property "ID_Start"

    UnicodeIDContinue::
      any Unicode code point with the Unicode property "ID_Continue"


    NOTE 3
    The sets of code points with Unicode properties "ID_Start" and
    "ID_Continue" include, respectively, the code points with Unicode
    properties "Other_ID_Start" and "Other_ID_Continue".

---*/


var C = class {
  static #$; static #_; static #\u{6F}; static #\u2118; static #ZW_\u200C_NJ; static #ZW_\u200D_J
  m() { return 42; }
  static $(value) {
    this.#$ = value;
    return this.#$;
  }
  static _(value) {
    this.#_ = value;
    return this.#_;
  }
  static \u{6F}(value) {
    this.#\u{6F} = value;
    return this.#\u{6F};
  }
  static \u2118(value) {
    this.#\u2118 = value;
    return this.#\u2118;
  }
  static ZW_\u200C_NJ(value) {
    this.#ZW_\u200C_NJ = value;
    return this.#ZW_\u200C_NJ;
  }
  static ZW_\u200D_J(value) {
    this.#ZW_\u200D_J = value;
    return this.#ZW_\u200D_J;
  }
}

var c = new C();

assert.sameValue(c.m(), 42);
assert.sameValue(c.m, C.prototype.m);
assert(
  !Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(c, "m"),
  "m doesn't appear as an own property on the C instance"
);

verifyProperty(C.prototype, "m", {
  enumerable: false,
  configurable: true,
  writable: true,
});

assert.sameValue(C.$(1), 1);
assert.sameValue(C._(1), 1);
assert.sameValue(C.\u{6F}(1), 1);
assert.sameValue(C.\u2118(1), 1);
assert.sameValue(C.ZW_\u200C_NJ(1), 1);
assert.sameValue(C.ZW_\u200D_J(1), 1);

test262-language--expressions--dynamic-import--import-attributes--2nd-param-with-enumeration

Expected: valid; goal: script

// Copyright (C) 2021 the V8 project authors. All rights reserved.
// This code is governed by the BSD license found in the LICENSE file.
/*---
description: >
  Follows the semantics of the EnumerableOwnPropertyNames abstract operation
  during attributes enumeration
esid: sec-import-call-runtime-semantics-evaluation
info: |
  2.1.1.1 EvaluateImportCall ( specifierExpression [ , optionsExpression ] )
    [...]
    6. Let promiseCapability be ! NewPromiseCapability(%Promise%).
    7. Let specifierString be ToString(specifier).
    8. IfAbruptRejectPromise(specifierString, promiseCapability).
    9. Let assertions be a new empty List.
    10. If options is not undefined, then
        a. If Type(options) is not Object,
           [...]
        b. Let assertionsObj be Get(options, "assert").
        c. IfAbruptRejectPromise(assertionsObj, promiseCapability).
        d. If assertionsObj is not undefined,
           i. If Type(assertionsObj) is not Object,
              [...]
           ii. Let keys be EnumerableOwnPropertyNames(assertionsObj, key).
    [...]
features: [dynamic-import, import-attributes, Symbol, Proxy]
flags: [async]
---*/

var symbol = Symbol('');
var target = {
  [symbol]: '',
  unreported: '',
  nonEnumerable: ''
};
var descriptors = {
  [symbol]: {configurable: true, enumerable: true},
  nonEnumerable: {configurable: true, enumerable: false}
};

var options = {
  with: new Proxy({}, {
    ownKeys: function() {
      return [symbol, 'nonEnumerable', 'absent'];
    },
    get() {
      throw new Error("Should not be called");
    },
    getOwnPropertyDescriptor(target, name) {
      return descriptors[name];
    }
  })
};

import('./2nd-param_FIXTURE.js', options)
  .then(function(module) {
    assert.sameValue(module.default, 262);
  })
  .then($DONE, $DONE);

All source units, expected labels and observed outcomes—not just the examples—are linked below.

HTML relevance of Tree-sitter's three false rejections: they involve a hashbang terminated by U+2028, a carriage-return-containing comment that permits automatic semicolon insertion, and a sloppy arrow parameter named yield. Normal HTML preprocessing converts CR to LF before JavaScript parsing, so the raw-CR fixture is a parser-conformance edge case rather than evidence that your HTML pipeline will produce that failure. Hashbang placement is also sensitive to leading text/newlines. These caveats do not change the raw-JavaScript labels, but they limit the inference about real generated HTML.

03 / What “thorough” means here

Reproducible backtesting, not a completeness claim.

  • Seven pinned configurations run in separate fresh Linux/Python 3.13 jobs, on the same frozen source corpus.
  • Official Test262 provenance preserves source path, commit, original-source hashes, chosen parse goal, strictness transformation and exclusion rules. See the full manifest rather than assuming every upstream test is eligible.
  • Only parse-phase negatives are labeled invalid. Runtime failures and missing host functions do not make source syntax-invalid. Resolution negatives and out-of-contract proposals are excluded by the corpus builder.
  • Each parser runs in a spawned worker. A five-second external deadline bounds each call; a failed worker is retired. Inconclusive outcomes are reported separately and do not become successful invalid detection.
  • Script and Module are not interchangeable. The fork and Acorn explicitly use ES2025; other packages use their shipped grammar and are evaluated against the same labels, with that mismatch disclosed.
  • All Esprima distributions are installed in separate environments because they share the esprima import name.
  • The initial independent-context proposals were challenged by corpus, scope, specification, maintenance and final-article reviewers. They use the same model family; this is not cross-model proof or a popularity vote.

The 500-file standards subset is deterministic, not random production traffic. Feature families and fixtures are correlated. The study does not establish a production false-positive probability, exhaust all ECMAScript syntax, or constitute a full Test262 run. It is also not a “backtest” of historical LLM output: no representative user production dataset was supplied. For deployment, add your actual model's accepted and rejected outputs as a regression set.

The pinned snapshot is May 31, 2025, with a conservative feature policy—not a complete final-edition inventory. The subset spans 195 directories but favors richly subdivided class/generator/destructuring families; only 24 of its 500 samples use Module. Feature tags are metadata, not proof that a syntax form occurs in the outer program (a RegExp-constructor test may contain no regex literal). The original modern-syntax cases complement this limitation; neither subset alone establishes exhaustive feature coverage. An independent reviewer checked all 500 labels, transformations and source/body hashes against the pinned archive.

04 / Deployment reality

Correctness, startup, memory and failure modes.

CandidateAdapter init msWorker peak RSS MiBDepth 100Depth 300112,779-byte valid program
acorn-v8318.0102.8validvalidvalid; 15.6 ms
acorn-quickjs23.832.7validresource_limitvalid; 149.9 ms
esprima-fork8.433.3resource_limitresource_limitvalid; 185.6 ms
esprima232.637.0validator_failurevalidator_failurevalid; 92.7 ms
tree-sitter1.132.7validvalidvalid; 16.3 ms
oxc-python0.832.8validvalidvalid; 3.3 ms
esprima292.8132.0validator_failurevalidator_failurevalid; 109.9 ms

Worker parse median includes parser/binding verdict conversion but excludes parent/child IPC. Adapter initialization includes imports and trusted-parser setup inside the worker, not interpreter process launch. Peak RSS is the Linux worker's process high-water mark across the run, including imports and earlier tests; it is not per-input allocation or a hard memory cap. Size probes are single observations, not stable throughput estimates. Jobs run on separate hosted runners, so timings are approximate; hardware variance matters.

Three valid nesting probes (32, 100, 300) and three valid flat-program size probes (929, 10,279, 112,779 bytes) are separate from the 607 classification samples. Warm end-to-end repeated measurements and raw timings are retained in the evidence file. Concurrency scaling, long-running leaks, browser compatibility and hardened sandboxing were not certified. Production workers should also have OS/container memory limits.

Depth limits were measured under the tested defaults. QuickJS-NG exposes stack-size configuration; its depth-300 result is not proof that it can never handle that source. We did not tune stack budgets across hosts. This is a default-configuration comparison, not an optimized engine shootout.

Where each approach fits

  • Tree-sitter: fast, mature parsing ecosystem; helpful recovery and source ranges. It includes JSX and omits many early-error rules. A clean tree is not a browser-validity guarantee.
  • Original Esprima: stale modern-syntax coverage; false rejections make it a poor fit here.
  • Modern Esprima fork: strongest tested pure-Python strict option; smaller adoption and depth constraints.
  • esprima2: portable but misses scope/context errors found by the stronger validators.
  • Oxc Python wrapper: fast native parser; the inspected v0.1.1 binding omits Oxc's semantic syntax-checking pass. Its results do not evaluate the full Oxc toolchain.
  • Acorn: modern grammar, source-goal and early-error checking; needs a vendored trusted bundle plus a Python engine wheel. Embedded engine execution is only for the parser.
05 / Maintenance and adoption

Evaluate the package you deploy—not just its famous core.

ComponentStars snapshotMonthly downloadsRelease/maintenance observation
Tree-sitter core / Python binding27,465 / 1,52248,642,917 (binding)Binding released Nov 9, 2026
JavaScript grammar49816,220,142Released Sep 1, 2025; repository newer than shipped wheel
Acorn11,4761,297,676,177 (npm)8.19.0 Oct 5, 2026; several releases during 2026
esprima-fork01295.0.0 Oct 13, 2026; previous release 2022
esprima27321,7416.0.0 Apr 22, 2026
Original Python Esprima2651,514,617Last package release Aug 2018
Oxc core / Python wrapper23,454 / 049 (wrapper)Wrapper Nov 2025; core cadence does not update wrapper
quickjs-ng Python wrapper10553,556Nov 5, 2026; recurring recent releases
mini-racer Python wrapper2192,293,645Feb 1, 2026; roughly nine months without a package release

Retrieved November 15, 2026. GitHub API and registry metadata are primary sources. PyPI Stats is a third-party download aggregation service; npm's endpoint reports October 16–November 14. PyPI Stats' last_month responses do not provide identical window boundaries. These totals count automation, transitive installs and repeated downloads—not unique users, latest-version adoption, successful deployments or correctness. Stars are corroborating attention, not a safety or reliability certificate.

QuickJS-NG's engine has 4,152 stars but its Python wrapper has 10; do not merge them. V8's reputation likewise does not prove the freshness of mini-racer's bundled build. Wheel availability is verified from filenames, but only Linux x86-64 was benchmarked here. The original Windows machine blocked some native imports through Application Control; Linux CI avoided that local restriction.

All exact endpoints, timestamps, release samples, wheel coverage, licenses and raw download responses: maintenance evidence report. No vulnerability audit, maintainer response-time study or supply-chain certification is claimed.

Decision rule

  1. Correct context and honest failure classification first.
  2. Minimize false rejections on the valid sample set.
  3. Among comparably low-false-alarm choices, prefer useful invalid detection.
  4. Then weigh deployability, operational limits and maintenance/adoption.

There is no arbitrary weighted “stars score.” Acorn's overall recommendation is a disclosed engineering judgment: strong validation plus established parser adoption, at the cost of a native engine dependency, startup overhead, wrapper maintenance and a known missed edge case. If pure Python is mandatory, use the fork instead; if only advisory structural feedback is needed, Tree-sitter remains reasonable.

06 / Tested integration

The recommended Python snippet.

python -m pip install mini-racer==0.14.1

Vendor Acorn 8.19.0's prebuilt bundle as acorn.js alongside this module and retain its MIT license. The snippet verifies the exact bundle hash. No npm or Node installation is needed in the application container. The tested API uses Python ≥3.10 with a compatible native wheel.

"""Node-free strict syntax check: only trusted Acorn code executes."""
import hashlib
from pathlib import Path

from py_mini_racer import MiniRacer

ACORN_SHA256 = "b95392d79457d0357555c5ed3e56302d074b92dff119bcf3b44179500b10e9df"
WRAPPER = """
function checkJavaScript(source, mode) {
  try {
    acorn.parse(source, {ecmaVersion: 2025, sourceType: mode});
    return {status: "valid"};
  } catch (error) {
    if (error instanceof SyntaxError &&
        !error.message.startsWith("Not enough stack space")) {
      return {status: "invalid", message: error.message,
              line: error.loc.line, utf16Column: error.loc.column};
    }
    return {status: "inconclusive", reason: "parser_resource_or_internal_error"};
  }
}
"""


class JavaScriptValidator:
    """Use one context per worker; close it when that worker is retired."""
    def __init__(self, acorn_path: str):
        bundle = Path(acorn_path).read_bytes()
        if hashlib.sha256(bundle).hexdigest() != ACORN_SHA256:
            raise ValueError("Unexpected Acorn bundle: expected pinned 8.19.0")
        self.context = MiniRacer()
        try:
            self.context.eval(bundle.decode("utf-8"))
            self.context.eval(WRAPPER)
        except Exception:
            self.context.close()
            raise

    def check(self, source: str, mode: str = "script") -> dict:
        if mode not in ("script", "module"):
            raise ValueError("Use the actual script or module parse goal")
        if len(source.encode("utf-8")) > 256_000:
            return {"status": "inconclusive", "reason": "input_size_limit"}
        try:
            # Pass source as an argument; never interpolate it into eval().
            return self.context.call("checkJavaScript", source, mode, timeout=2000)
        except Exception as exc:
            return {"status": "inconclusive", "reason": type(exc).__name__}

    def close(self):
        self.context.close()

validator = JavaScriptValidator("acorn.js")
try:
    result = validator.check(extracted_script, mode="module")  # actual HTML type
finally:
    validator.close()

Reuse one context per worker rather than initializing on every request. The 256,000-byte input cap and two-second embedded deadline are example policies, not language rules. The external worker deadline used in the benchmark is additional to this compact helper; use process-level memory limits and deadlines for a service. Infrastructure exceptions remain inconclusive. SyntaxError locations use one-based lines and zero-based UTF-16 columns. Do not append those directly to Python scalar or UTF-8 byte offsets.

The integration checks verify modern syntax, duplicate bindings, module/script await, size handling, deep input, JSX rejection and the documented edge-case miss. A sentinel check confirms submitted assignment code is parsed without being executed. Acorn can construct input-derived RegExp objects while building its AST; no matches or submitted statements are run. Parsing is still resource-consuming native work.

Optional Tree-sitter advisory screen
"""Low-noise structural screening, not complete ECMAScript validation."""
from tree_sitter import Language, Parser
import tree_sitter_javascript


class JavaScriptScreen:
    def __init__(self):
        self.parser = Parser(Language(tree_sitter_javascript.language()))

    def check(self, source: str) -> dict:
        if len(source.encode("utf-8")) > 256_000:
            return {"status": "inconclusive", "reason": "input_size_limit"}
        tree = self.parser.parse(source.encode("utf-8"))
        if not tree.root_node.has_error:
            return {"status": "no_structural_error_found", "issues": []}
        issues = []
        pending = [tree.root_node]
        while pending and len(issues) < 20:
            node = pending.pop()
            if node.is_error or node.is_missing:
                issues.append({
                    "kind": "missing" if node.is_missing else "error",
                    "node_type": node.type,
                    "line": node.start_point.row + 1,
                    "byte_column": node.start_point.column,
                })
            # Include unnamed children: missing punctuation may be anonymous.
            pending.extend(reversed(node.children))
        return {"status": "needs_review", "issues": issues}

Inspect ERROR and MISSING nodes, including anonymous punctuation. Report “needs review,” not definitive invalidity. Do not build a homegrown ECMAScript semantic checker to patch every gap.

Keep the HTML boundary correct

Use your existing BeautifulSoup extraction deliberately: preserve raw script text, classify classic/module/data, and parse each source unit separately. External src is unchecked unless its source is supplied. JSON/JSON-LD requires a strict JSON parser; import maps and speculation rules need their own schema rules too.

A literal </script> can terminate HTML script content even inside a JavaScript string. Comment termination can leave a valid but unintentionally truncated program. HTML escaped/double-escaped states, noscript, templates, namespaces and handlers need correct context. Do not HTML-unescape script bodies. Event-handler attributes are different and can legally contain return.

Expanded boundary checks and exact observed outcomes: HTML/JSON regression results. There are 76 passing assertions and 4 context observations, measured separately on Windows/Python 3.14.8 with BeautifulSoup 4.15.0 and html5lib 1.1. Their scope and limitations are recorded; no full browser rendering test is claimed.

07 / Reproduce, inspect, challenge

Evidence is part of the article.

  • All 607 labeled samples

  • Pinned Test262 selection manifest and license
  • Parallel Linux workflow and logs
  • Repository
Canonical corpus SHA-256: e4f90c42007fe25aab4ff7d1094bc0b49f160ead3bd49e6edcc995571fc81e4d
Acorn asset SHA-256: b95392d79457d0357555c5ed3e56302d074b92dff119bcf3b44179500b10e9df

To rerun locally, save data/corpus.json as cases.json beside the harness below and the verified Acorn bundle. Do not add the old challenge files too: the downloadable corpus is already combined. Create a fresh environment per candidate and use the exact packages in the workflow. Run python benchmark.py acorn-v8, or another listed candidate. The workflow embeds compressed UTF-8 source data for reproducibility, then materializes it before parsing.

To regenerate the standards selection itself, save the 82 baseline cases as cases.json and the 25 scope cases as challenge-scopes.json beside the corpus builder—not the combined 607-case download. The builder verifies the pinned upstream archive hash and uses its metadata parser, retaining licenses and source provenance.

Benchmark harness
"""Measured parser comparison. Submitted source is passed as data, never run."""
import argparse
from datetime import datetime, timezone
import hashlib
import importlib.metadata
import json
import multiprocessing
from pathlib import Path
import platform
import statistics
import sys
import time

ROOT = Path(__file__).parent
WRAPPER = """
function validateSource(source, mode) {
  try {
    acorn.parse(source, {ecmaVersion: 2025, sourceType: mode});
    return {status: "valid"};
  } catch (e) {
    if (!(e instanceof SyntaxError)) throw e;
    if (e.message.startsWith("Not enough stack space to parse input")) {
      return {status: "resource_limit", diagnostic: e.message};
    }
    return {status: "invalid", diagnostic: e.message,
            line: e.loc.line, column: e.loc.column};
  }
}
"""


def adapter(name):
    if name in ("esprima", "esprima2", "esprima-fork"):
        import esprima
        options = {"tolerant": False, "jsx": False}
        if name == "esprima-fork":
            options["ecmaVersion"] = 2025
        def check(source, mode):
            try:
                method = esprima.parseModule if mode == "module" else esprima.parseScript
                method(source, options)
                return {"status": "valid"}
            except esprima.error_handler.Error as exc:
                if name == "esprima-fork" and "nesting exceeds the" in str(exc) and "resource limit" in str(exc):
                    return {"status": "resource_limit", "diagnostic": str(exc)}
                return {"status": "invalid", "diagnostic": str(exc)}
        return check, None
    if name == "tree-sitter":
        from tree_sitter import Language, Parser
        import tree_sitter_javascript
        parser = Parser(Language(tree_sitter_javascript.language()))
        def check(source, mode):
            tree = parser.parse(source.encode("utf-8"))
            return {"status": "invalid" if tree.root_node.has_error else "valid"}
        return check, None
    if name == "oxc-python":
        import oxc_python
        def check(source, mode):
            result = oxc_python.parse(source, source_type=mode)
            if result.panicked and not result.errors:
                return {"status": "validator_failure", "diagnostic": "Oxc parser panicked"}
            return {"status": "invalid" if result.errors else "valid"}
        return check, None
    if name == "acorn-v8":
        from py_mini_racer import MiniRacer
        context = MiniRacer()
        context.eval((ROOT / "acorn.js").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        context.eval(WRAPPER)
        return lambda source, mode: context.call("validateSource", source, mode, timeout=2000), context
    if name == "acorn-quickjs":
        import quickjs
        context = quickjs.Context()
        context.set_memory_limit(128 * 1024 * 1024)
        context.set_time_limit(2)
        context.eval((ROOT / "acorn.js").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        context.eval(WRAPPER)
        call = context.get("validateSource")
        return lambda source, mode: json.loads(call(source, mode).json()), None
    raise ValueError(name)


def worker(connection, name):
    """One native-parser process; parent owns the hard wall-clock deadline."""
    resource = None
    started = time.perf_counter()
    try:
        check, resource = adapter(name)
        connection.send({"status": "ready", "startup_ms": (time.perf_counter() - started) * 1000})
        while True:
            request = connection.recv()
            if request is None:
                break
            source, mode = request
            started = time.perf_counter_ns()
            try:
                verdict = check(source, mode)
            except BaseException as exc:
                verdict = {"status": "validator_failure", "diagnostic": f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"}
            verdict["parse_microseconds"] = (time.perf_counter_ns() - started) / 1000
            if sys.platform == "linux":
                import resource as os_resource
                verdict["worker_peak_rss_kib"] = os_resource.getrusage(os_resource.RUSAGE_SELF).ru_maxrss
            connection.send(verdict)
    except (EOFError, BrokenPipeError):
        pass
    except BaseException as exc:
        connection.send({"status": "unavailable", "reason": f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"})
    finally:
        if resource is not None:
            resource.close()
        connection.close()


class IsolatedChecker:
    def __init__(self, name):
        self.name = name
        self.process = None
        self.startup_ms = None
        self.start()

    def start(self):
        context = multiprocessing.get_context("spawn")
        self.connection, child = context.Pipe()
        self.process = context.Process(target=worker, args=(child, self.name), daemon=True)
        self.process.start()
        child.close()
        if not self.connection.poll(30):
            self.close()
            raise RuntimeError("Validator initialization timed out")
        ready = self.connection.recv()
        if ready["status"] != "ready":
            self.close()
            raise RuntimeError(ready.get("reason", "Initialization failed"))
        self.startup_ms = ready["startup_ms"]

    def __call__(self, source, mode):
        if self.process is None:
            self.start()
        try:
            self.connection.send((source, mode))
            if not self.connection.poll(5):
                self.close()
                return {"status": "timeout", "diagnostic": "5-second external worker deadline"}
            return self.connection.recv()
        except (EOFError, BrokenPipeError, OSError) as exc:
            self.close()
            return {"status": "validator_failure", "diagnostic": f"Worker exited: {type(exc).__name__}"}

    def close(self):
        if self.process is not None:
            self.process.terminate()
            self.process.join(5)
            if self.process.is_alive():
                self.process.kill()
                self.process.join()
            self.connection.close()
            self.process = None


def main():
    cli = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    cli.add_argument("candidate")
    args = cli.parse_args()
    cases = json.loads((ROOT / "cases.json").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    for extra in sorted(ROOT.glob("challenge-*.json")):
        cases.extend(json.loads(extra.read_text(encoding="utf-8")))
    assert len({case[0] for case in cases}) == len(cases), "Duplicate case IDs"
    result = {
        "candidate": args.candidate, "python": platform.python_version(),
        "platform": platform.platform(), "ecmascript_contract": 2025,
        "cases_sha256": hashlib.sha256(json.dumps(cases, ensure_ascii=False).encode()).hexdigest(),
        "case_count": len(cases),
        "timestamp_utc": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(),
        "benchmark_sha256": hashlib.sha256(Path(__file__).read_bytes()).hexdigest(),
        "acorn_sha256": hashlib.sha256((ROOT / "acorn.js").read_bytes()).hexdigest(),
        "acorn_version": "8.19.0",
        "edition_selection": "explicit 2025" if args.candidate in ("esprima-fork", "acorn-v8", "acorn-quickjs") else "not selected; package default grammar",
        "executable": sys.executable,
        "versions": {d.metadata["Name"]: d.version for d in importlib.metadata.distributions()},
        "isolation": "spawned worker; 5-second per-input external wall-clock deadline",
    }
    resource = None
    try:
        check = resource = IsolatedChecker(args.candidate)
        result["adapter_startup_ms"] = check.startup_ms
    except Exception as exc:
        result.update(status="unavailable", reason=f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}")
    else:
        rows = []
        for case_id, mode, expected, group, source in cases:
            try:
                actual = check(source, mode)
            except Exception as exc:
                actual = {"status": "validator_failure", "diagnostic": f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"}
            rows.append(dict(id=case_id, mode=mode, expected=expected, group=group, **actual))
        # Warm, tiny-source latency includes the binding and verdict conversion, not setup.
        durations = []
        timing_failure = None
        for _ in range(3):
            start = time.perf_counter_ns()
            attempts = 0
            for row, case in zip(rows, cases):
                if row["status"] in ("valid", "invalid"):
                    try:
                        check(case[4], case[1])
                        attempts += 1
                    except Exception as exc:
                        timing_failure = f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
            if attempts:
                durations.append((time.perf_counter_ns() - start) / attempts / 1000)
        resource_probes = []
        for depth in (32, 100, 300):
            try:
                probe = check("(" * depth + "0" + ")" * depth + ";", "script")
            except Exception as exc:
                probe = {"status": "validator_failure", "diagnostic": f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"}
            resource_probes.append({"depth": depth, "expected": True, **probe})
        size_probes = []
        for statements in (50, 500, 5000):
            source = "\n".join(f"var bench_{i} = {i};" for i in range(statements))
            try:
                probe = check(source, "script")
            except Exception as exc:
                probe = {"status": "validator_failure", "diagnostic": f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"}
            size_probes.append({"bytes": len(source.encode()), "expected": True, **probe})
        result.update(
            status="measured", cases=rows,
            false_accepts=[r["id"] for r in rows if not r["expected"] and r["status"] == "valid"],
            false_rejects=[r["id"] for r in rows if r["expected"] and r["status"] == "invalid"],
            failures=[r["id"] for r in rows if r["status"] == "validator_failure"],
            inconclusive=[r["id"] for r in rows if r["status"] not in ("valid", "invalid")],
            resource_probes=resource_probes,
            size_probes=size_probes,
            worker_peak_rss_kib=max(
                (r.get("worker_peak_rss_kib", 0) for r in rows + resource_probes + size_probes),
                default=0,
            ) or None,
            timing_failure=timing_failure,
            timed_case_count=sum(r["status"] in ("valid", "invalid") for r in rows),
            warm_microseconds_per_case_median=statistics.median(durations) if durations else None,
            parse_microseconds_median=statistics.median(
                r["parse_microseconds"] for r in rows if "parse_microseconds" in r
            ) if any("parse_microseconds" in r for r in rows) else None,
            valid_count=sum(case[2] for case in cases),
            invalid_count=sum(not case[2] for case in cases),
        )
    finally:
        if resource is not None:
            resource.close()
    path = ROOT / f"results-{args.candidate}.json"
    path.write_text(json.dumps(result, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
    print(json.dumps({k: v for k, v in result.items() if k not in ("cases", "versions")}, indent=2))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
Recommended snippet with executable self-checks
"""Node-free strict syntax check: only trusted Acorn code executes."""
import hashlib
from pathlib import Path

from py_mini_racer import MiniRacer

ACORN_SHA256 = "b95392d79457d0357555c5ed3e56302d074b92dff119bcf3b44179500b10e9df"
WRAPPER = """
function checkJavaScript(source, mode) {
  try {
    acorn.parse(source, {ecmaVersion: 2025, sourceType: mode});
    return {status: "valid"};
  } catch (error) {
    if (error instanceof SyntaxError &&
        !error.message.startsWith("Not enough stack space")) {
      return {status: "invalid", message: error.message,
              line: error.loc.line, utf16Column: error.loc.column};
    }
    return {status: "inconclusive", reason: "parser_resource_or_internal_error"};
  }
}
"""


class JavaScriptValidator:
    """Use one context per worker; close it when that worker is retired."""
    def __init__(self, acorn_path: str):
        bundle = Path(acorn_path).read_bytes()
        if hashlib.sha256(bundle).hexdigest() != ACORN_SHA256:
            raise ValueError("Unexpected Acorn bundle: expected pinned 8.19.0")
        self.context = MiniRacer()
        try:
            self.context.eval(bundle.decode("utf-8"))
            self.context.eval(WRAPPER)
        except Exception:
            self.context.close()
            raise

    def check(self, source: str, mode: str = "script") -> dict:
        if mode not in ("script", "module"):
            raise ValueError("Use the actual script or module parse goal")
        if len(source.encode("utf-8")) > 256_000:
            return {"status": "inconclusive", "reason": "input_size_limit"}
        try:
            # Pass source as an argument; never interpolate it into eval().
            return self.context.call("checkJavaScript", source, mode, timeout=2000)
        except Exception as exc:
            return {"status": "inconclusive", "reason": type(exc).__name__}

    def close(self):
        self.context.close()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    validator = JavaScriptValidator(str(Path(__file__).with_name("acorn.js")))
    try:
        assert validator.check("const x = obj?.x ?? 0;")["status"] == "valid"
        assert validator.check("let x; let x;")["status"] == "invalid"
        assert validator.check("await 1;", "module")["status"] == "valid"
        assert validator.check("await 1;", "script")["status"] == "invalid"
        assert validator.check("while (true) {}")["status"] == "valid"
        assert validator.check("globalThis.userCodeExecuted = true;")["status"] == "valid"
        assert validator.context.eval("typeof userCodeExecuted") == "undefined"
        assert validator.check("(" * 300 + "0" + ")" * 300 + ";")["status"] == "valid"
        assert validator.check(" " * 256_001)["status"] == "inconclusive"
        assert validator.check("const el = <div/>;")["status"] == "invalid"
        # Known pinned-version miss is documented, not silently patched.
        assert validator.check("class C { static { const f = () => arguments; } }")["status"] == "valid"
        print("11 Acorn integration checks passed; submitted code remained inert.")
    finally:
        validator.close()
Standards corpus builder
"""Build a deterministic, metadata-labelled Test262 syntax corpus; never run JS."""

from collections import Counter, defaultdict
import hashlib
import importlib.util
import json
from pathlib import Path
import tarfile
import urllib.request


ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
CACHE = ROOT / "test262-cache"
COMMIT = "5c9ff876dcefd19a27ef735224bd69ac1d1ee8e9"
ARCHIVE_SHA256 = "ad4ee27ecf513e71bbd8b4fae5a4a6c6a407839503ea397461cacdc32accdbe2"
ARCHIVE_URL = f"https://codeload.github.com/tc39/test262/tar.gz/{COMMIT}"
BASE_URL = f"https://github.com/tc39/test262/blob/{COMMIT}"
PER_LABEL = 250


def sha256(data):
    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()


def download():
    CACHE.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    archive = CACHE / f"{COMMIT}.tar.gz"
    if not archive.exists():
        with urllib.request.urlopen(ARCHIVE_URL, timeout=120) as response:
            archive.write_bytes(response.read())
    if sha256(archive.read_bytes()) != ARCHIVE_SHA256:
        raise ValueError("Pinned Test262 archive checksum mismatch")
    return archive


def fail(message):
    raise ValueError(message)


def load_parser(archive):
    """Use the pinned upstream metadata parser, not a home-grown YAML subset."""
    prefix = f"test262-{COMMIT}/"
    support = {}
    for name in ("LICENSE", "features.txt", "tools/packaging/parseTestRecord.py",
                 "tools/packaging/monkeyYaml.py"):
        data = archive.extractfile(prefix + name).read()
        target = CACHE / name
        target.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        target.write_bytes(data)
        support[name] = {"sha256": sha256(data), "url": f"{BASE_URL}/{name}"}
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(
        "test262_metadata", CACHE / "tools/packaging/parseTestRecord.py"
    )
    parser = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    spec.loader.exec_module(parser)
    return parser, support


def strict_source(source):
    # A hashbang must stay the first line even in a strict-script variant.
    if source.startswith("#!"):
        head, separator, rest = source.partition("\n")
        return head + separator + '"use strict";\n' + rest
    return '"use strict";\n' + source


def variant(source, flags, path):
    if "module" in flags:
        return "module", source, "module (intrinsically strict)"
    if "raw" in flags:
        return "script", source, "raw (no transformation)"
    if "onlyStrict" in flags:
        return "script", strict_source(source), "onlyStrict prefix"
    if "noStrict" in flags:
        return "script", source, "noStrict"
    # Test262 ordinarily runs both: retain just one, never inflate by variants.
    if int(sha256(path.encode())[:2], 16) % 2:
        return "script", strict_source(source), "default strict variant"
    return "script", source, "default non-strict variant"


def self_check():
    assert variant("with (x) {}", ["noStrict"], "x")[1] == "with (x) {}"
    assert variant("x", ["onlyStrict"], "x")[1] == '"use strict";\nx'
    assert variant("x", ["module"], "x")[0] == "module"
    assert variant("#!x\nx", ["raw"], "x")[1] == "#!x\nx"
    assert strict_source("#!x\nx") == '#!x\n"use strict";\nx'


def main():
    self_check()
    archive_path = download()
    exclusions = Counter()
    excluded_features = Counter()
    pools = {True: defaultdict(list), False: defaultdict(list)}
    with tarfile.open(archive_path) as archive:
        parser, support = load_parser(archive)
        feature_text = (CACHE / "features.txt").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
        standard_section = feature_text.split("## Standard language features")[1]
        standard_section = standard_section.split("## Test-Harness Features")[0]
        allowed = {
            line.split("#")[0].strip() for line in standard_section.splitlines()
            if line.split("#")[0].strip()
        }
        # Already standard in ES2025 despite still being in the proposal section
        # of this May snapshot. All other proposal-section flags stay excluded.
        allowed.add("regexp-duplicate-named-groups")
        seen_bodies = set()
        for member in sorted(archive.getmembers(), key=lambda item: item.name):
            path = member.name.partition("/")[2]
            if not member.isfile() or not path.endswith(".js"):
                continue
            if not path.startswith(("test/language/", "test/annexB/language/",
                                    "test/built-ins/RegExp/")):
                exclusions["outside_language_annexB_regexp_scope"] += 1
                continue
            if "_FIXTURE" in path:
                exclusions["fixture"] += 1
                continue
            if member.size > 100_000:
                exclusions["source_exceeds_100KB"] += 1
                continue
            original = archive.extractfile(member).read()
            text = original.decode("utf-8")
            try:
                metadata = parser.parseTestRecord(text, path, onerror=fail)
            except (ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError):
                exclusions["metadata_parse_or_license_error"] += 1
                continue
            flags = metadata.get("flags", [])
            features = metadata.get("features", [])
            unknown = set(features) - allowed
            if unknown:
                exclusions["feature_not_in_es2025_allowlist"] += 1
                excluded_features.update(unknown)
                continue
            if set(flags) - {"onlyStrict", "noStrict", "module", "raw",
                             "async", "generated", "CanBlockIsFalse", "CanBlockIsTrue"}:
                exclusions["unsupported_flags"] += 1
                continue
            if sum(flag in flags for flag in ("onlyStrict", "noStrict", "module", "raw")) > 1:
                exclusions["conflicting_goal_flags"] += 1
                continue
            negative = metadata.get("negative")
            if negative and (not isinstance(negative, dict)
                             or negative.get("phase") not in ("parse", "runtime")):
                exclusions["resolution_or_unknown_negative_phase"] += 1
                continue
            expected = not negative or negative["phase"] != "parse"
            if not expected and negative.get("type") != "SyntaxError":
                exclusions["parse_negative_not_SyntaxError"] += 1
                continue
            # De-duplicate before adding directives, ignoring frontmatter/license.
            body = metadata["test"]
            body_key = body.strip()
            if body_key in seen_bodies:
                exclusions["duplicate_untransformed_body"] += 1
                continue
            seen_bodies.add(body_key)
            mode, source, transformation = variant(text, flags, path)
            directory = str(Path(path).parent).replace("\\", "/")
            # Fine-grained syntax directories and feature combinations form strata.
            stratum = directory + "|" + ",".join(sorted(features))
            record = {
                "id": "test262-" + path.removeprefix("test/").removesuffix(".js").replace("/", "--"),
                "upstream_path": path,
                "source_url": f"{BASE_URL}/{path}",
                "raw_url": f"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tc39/test262/{COMMIT}/{path}",
                "upstream_sha256": sha256(original),
                "source_sha256": sha256(source.encode("utf-8")),
                "body_sha256": sha256(body_key.encode("utf-8")),
                "mode": mode,
                "expected": expected,
                "flags": flags,
                "features": features,
                "negative": negative,
                "esid": metadata.get("esid"),
                "transformation": transformation,
                "stratum": stratum,
                "label_basis": "negative.phase=parse, type=SyntaxError" if not expected
                else "negative.phase=runtime (outer source parses)" if negative
                else "positive Test262 test (outer source parses)",
            }
            pools[bool(expected)][stratum].append((record, source, original))

        selected = []
        for expected in (True, False):
            buckets = pools[expected]
            keys = sorted(buckets, key=lambda key: sha256(key.encode()))
            for values in buckets.values():
                values.sort(key=lambda item: sha256(item[0]["upstream_path"].encode()))
            picked = []
            if expected:
                # Explicitly exercise runtime-negative interpretation as well.
                for key in keys:
                    runtime = next((item for item in buckets[key]
                                    if (item[0]["negative"] or {}).get("phase") == "runtime"), None)
                    if runtime:
                        picked.append(runtime)
                        buckets[key].remove(runtime)
                        if len(picked) == 5:
                            break
                assert len(picked) == 5
            while len(picked) < PER_LABEL:
                progress = False
                for key in keys:
                    if buckets[key]:
                        picked.append(buckets[key].pop(0))
                        progress = True
                        if len(picked) == PER_LABEL:
                            break
                if not progress:
                    raise ValueError(f"Not enough candidates for expected={expected}")
            selected.extend(picked)

    selected.sort(key=lambda item: item[0]["id"])
    rows = []
    for record, source, original in selected:
        rows.append([record["id"], record["mode"], bool(record["expected"]),
                     "standards", source])
        target = CACHE / record["upstream_path"]
        target.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        target.write_bytes(original)
    assert len(rows) == 500
    assert len({row[0] for row in rows}) == len(rows)
    assert len({record["upstream_path"] for record, _, _ in selected}) == len(rows)
    assert len({(row[1], row[4]) for row in rows}) == len(rows)
    assert len({record["body_sha256"] for record, _, _ in selected}) == len(rows)
    existing = json.loads((ROOT / "cases.json").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    existing += json.loads((ROOT / "challenge-scopes.json").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    assert not {(row[1], row[4]) for row in rows} & {(row[1], row[4]) for row in existing}
    corpus_data = (json.dumps(rows, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2) + "\n").encode("utf-8")
    (ROOT / "challenge-standards.json").write_bytes(corpus_data)
    manifest = {
        "upstream_repository": "https://github.com/tc39/test262",
        "upstream_commit": COMMIT,
        "upstream_commit_date": "2025-05-31T18:46:32Z",
        "commit_lookup_url": "https://api.github.com/repos/tc39/test262/commits?until=2025-06-01T00:00:00Z&per_page=1",
        "archive_url": ARCHIVE_URL,
        "archive_sha256": sha256(archive_path.read_bytes()),
        "license_url": f"{BASE_URL}/LICENSE",
        "license_local": "test262-cache/LICENSE",
        "support_files": support,
        "ecmascript_contract": 2025,
        "edition_reference": "https://262.ecma-international.org/16.0/",
        "feature_policy": "Pinned standard-language section of features.txt plus ES2025 regexp-duplicate-named-groups; every other feature excluded.",
        "allowed_features": sorted(allowed),
        "selection": "250 per label; deterministic SHA256-ordered directory+feature strata, round robin; SHA256-ordered paths within each stratum; reserve five valid slots for runtime-negative tests from distinct strata; one variant per distinct upstream body.",
        "source_policy": "Original source including license and metadata; prepend use-strict only for strict variants (after hashbang if present). No harness injection or test execution.",
        "label_policy": "Only parse/SyntaxError negatives are invalid; positive and runtime negatives are syntax-valid; resolution and unknown negative phases excluded.",
        "corpus_sha256": sha256(corpus_data),
        "count": len(rows),
        "unique_upstream_files": len({record["upstream_path"] for record, _, _ in selected}),
        "unique_source_goal_pairs": len({(row[1], row[4]) for row in rows}),
        "maximum_source_bytes": max(len(row[4].encode("utf-8")) for row in rows),
        "selected_directory_count": len({record["upstream_path"].rsplit("/", 1)[0]
                                         for record, _, _ in selected}),
        "labels": dict(Counter("valid" if row[2] else "invalid" for row in rows)),
        "goals": dict(Counter(row[1] for row in rows)),
        "negative_phases": dict(Counter(
            (record["negative"] or {}).get("phase", "positive") for record, _, _ in selected
        )),
        "transformations": dict(Counter(record["transformation"] for record, _, _ in selected)),
        "selected_directories": dict(Counter(
            record["upstream_path"].rsplit("/", 1)[0] for record, _, _ in selected
        )),
        "selected_features": dict(Counter(
            feature for record, _, _ in selected for feature in record["features"]
        )),
        "exclusions": dict(exclusions),
        "excluded_features": dict(excluded_features),
        "limits": [
            "A stratified syntax sample, not full Test262 conformance or exhaustive ES2025 coverage.",
            "Metadata is the oracle; no validator outputs are used to choose labels or cases.",
            "Runtime assertions, eval strings, RegExp constructor strings and imported fixtures are not executed or validated recursively.",
            "Positive labels assert only outer-source parsing; runtime success and module resolution are outside scope.",
            "One strict/non-strict variant per upstream file; not both variants of the whole Test262 suite.",
            "Proposal tests without correct upstream feature annotations cannot be mechanically identified.",
            "Includes Annex B web-legacy syntax; hosts omitting optional Annex B grammar may differ.",
        ],
        "cases": [record for record, _, _ in selected],
    }
    (ROOT / "standards-manifest.json").write_text(
        json.dumps(manifest, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8"
    )
    print(json.dumps({key: manifest[key] for key in
                      ("count", "labels", "goals", "negative_phases", "transformations",
                       "selected_features", "exclusions")}, indent=2))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
HTML boundary test code
"""HTML extraction boundary regressions; never execute submitted JavaScript."""

import json
import platform

REFERENCES = {

    ("raw-named-entity", '<script>let s="&amp;";</script>', ['let s="&amp;";']),

    ("null-replacement", '<script>A\x00B</script>', ["A\ufffdB"]),
    ("unicode-source", '<script>const café="雪😀";\u2028let π=1;</script>', ['const café="雪😀";\u2028let π=1;']),
    ("json-literal-close", '<script type="application/json">{"s":"</script>"}</script>', ['{"s":"']),
    ("textarea-not-script", '<textarea><script>A</script></textarea>', []),
    ("comment-not-script", '<!-- <script>A</script> -->', []),

HANDLERS = [
    ("handler-ampersand", '<button onclick="return x &amp;&amp; y">', "return x && y"),
    ("handler-quotes", '<button onclick="return &quot;雪&quot;">', 'return "雪"'),
    ("handler-numeric", '<button ONCLICK="return x &#60; 2">', "return x < 2"),
    ("handler-decode-once", '<button onclick="return &amp;lt;">', "return &lt;"),
]


def reject_constant(value: str) -> None:
    raise ValueError(f"Non-JSON constant: {value}")


def sources(soup: BeautifulSoup) -> list[str]:
    return [str(tag.string) if tag.string is not None else "" for tag in soup.find_all("script")]


def main() -> None:
    results = []

    def record(name, category, html, expected, actual, **extra):
        results.append({
            "name": name, "category": category, "html": html,
            "expected": expected, "actual": actual,
            "status": "passed" if actual == expected else "failed", **extra,
        })

    for name, html, expected in EXTRACTION:
        record(name, "extraction", html, expected, sources(BeautifulSoup(html, "html5lib")))

    for name, attributes, expected_attrs, classification in ATTRIBUTES:
        html = f"<script {attributes}>SOURCE</script>"
        soup = BeautifulSoup(html, "html5lib")
        record(
            name, "attribute-boundary", html,
            {"sources": ["SOURCE"], "attributes": expected_attrs},
            {"sources": sources(soup), "attributes": dict(soup.script.attrs)},
            normative_classification=classification,
            classification_tested=False,
        )

    for name, source, valid, value in JSON_CASES:
        html = f'<script type="application/json">{source}</script>'
        extracted = sources(BeautifulSoup(html, "html5lib"))
        try:
            parsed = json.loads(extracted[0], parse_constant=reject_constant)
        except ValueError as exc:
            actual = {"sources": extracted, "valid": False}
            diagnostic = str(exc)
        else:
            actual = {"sources": extracted, "valid": True, "value": parsed}
            diagnostic = None
        expected = {"sources": [source], "valid": valid}
        if valid:
            expected["value"] = value
        record(f"json-{name}", "strict-json", html, expected, actual, diagnostic=diagnostic)

    for name, html, expected in HANDLERS:
        soup = BeautifulSoup(html, "html5lib")
        record(name, "event-attribute", html, expected, soup.button["onclick"])

    # These trees are observations, deliberately excluded from passing assertions.
    for name, html, caveat in [
        ("template", '<template><script>A</script></template><script>B</script>',
         "Browser template contents are a separate inert DocumentFragment; soup.find_all is not a browser document query."),
        ("template-table", '<template><table><script>A</script></table></template>',
         "html5lib 1.1 template tree building is not a browser-equivalence oracle."),
        ("noscript-body", '<body><noscript><script>A</script></noscript><script>B</script>',
         "BeautifulSoup uses html5lib default scripting=False; scripting-enabled body noscript is raw text."),
        ("noscript-head", '<head><noscript><script>A</script></noscript></head>',
         "Head noscript also depends on scripting flag and insertion mode; record both parser modes without browser claims."),
    ]:
        modes = {}
        for scripting in (False, True):
            tree = html5lib.parse(html, treebuilder="etree", namespaceHTMLElements=False, scripting=scripting)
            modes[str(scripting).lower()] = [node.text or "" for node in tree.iter("script")]
        results.append({
            "name": name, "category": "context-observation", "html": html,
            "status": "observed", "beautifulsoup_sources": sources(BeautifulSoup(html, "html5lib")),
            "html5lib_sources_by_scripting_flag": modes, "caveat": caveat,
        })

    counts = Counter(row["status"] for row in results)
    report = {
        "study": "Expanded HTML extraction boundary checks",
        "command": "research/env-main/Scripts/python research/html_expanded_checks.py",
        "environment": {
            "python": platform.python_version(),
            "beautifulsoup4": version("beautifulsoup4"), "html5lib": version("html5lib"),
        },
        "references": REFERENCES,
        "summary": {
            "total_cases": len(results), "asserted_cases": counts["passed"] + counts["failed"],
            "passed": counts["passed"], "failed": counts["failed"], "observations": counts["observed"],
            "by_category": dict(Counter(row["category"] for row in results)),
        },
        "scope_caveats": [
            "No JavaScript execution, network fetching, browser automation, rendering, or browser conformance certification.",
            "These HTML boundary cases complement, and are not counted as, ECMAScript parser benchmark cases.",
            "Classification labels are WHATWG reference facts; only attribute/source extraction is asserted, not a duplicated classifier or browser preparation.",
            "src presence (including empty) prevents treating body text as inline fallback for classic/module scripts.",
            "BeautifulSoup html5lib defaults to scripting=False; explicit html5lib scripting=True only changes parsing, never executes scripts.",
            "Template and noscript tree results are observations, not assertions of browser DOM equivalence.",
            "Event handler attributes are decoded once and need handler-specific compilation context; they are not standalone script units.",
            "JSON checks reject nonstandard constants and malformed syntax; they do not validate import-map/speculation-rule schemas or enforce unique object keys.",
            "Input is Unicode text, not a byte-stream encoding detection test; no claim about every malformed HTML, SVG/XML, CSP, or dynamic DOM case.",
        ],
        "results": results,
    }
    output = Path(__file__).with_name("html-expanded-results.json")
    output.write_text(json.dumps(report, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
    print(json.dumps(report["summary"], indent=2))
    for row in results:
        if row["status"] == "failed":
            print(json.dumps(row, ensure_ascii=False))
    if counts["failed"]:
        raise SystemExit(1)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Primary technical references

  • Test262 interpretation rules and ECMAScript 2025.
  • Pinned Acorn API/source, mini-racer, QuickJS-NG Python binding.
  • Pinned Tree-sitter JavaScript grammar.
  • Fork's own conformance and limitations; these are maintainer claims, not our full-suite measurements.
  • Oxc binding v0.1.1 and parser versus semantic checks.
  • HTML script processing and tokenization states.
Conclusion: choose Acorn-in-Python for the best measured balance of low false alarms, useful error detection and established adoption. Choose the modern fork when pure Python outweighs maintenance uncertainty. Choose Tree-sitter when advisory structural screening is enough. Keep resource failures inconclusive, and validate against your actual generated outputs before treating any measured rate as a production expectation.
Finite, versioned evidence—not a promise that accepted JavaScript renders correctly or that every possible invalid program is rejected. Repository contains the article, supporting research data and reproduction workflow, not an application.
